## Document 053: 2028 Should Be A Good Year

Source PDF: all report pdf/2028 Should Be A Good Year.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-07

Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: 2028 Should Be A Good Year
Date:    7 hours ago
2028 Should Be A Good Year

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
2028 Should Be A Good Year
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 6 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Patrick Fore / Unsplash
I. Blue skies ahead for earnings?
We are now seven years into our Roaring 2020s thesis, which remains
our base case for the rest of the decade, and it has played out very well
so far. When we first introduced the idea in August 2020, the prospect of
another Roaring '20s sounded a bit delusional. We argued that, as in the

### Page 3

1920s, rapidly proliferating technologies would drive a productivity boom
that would support stronger economic growth while containing inflation,
boosting real wages, widening profit margins, and lifting corporate
earnings.
Since the beginning of the current decade, the economy's resilience has
repeatedly defied the naysayers, as productivity growth has increased and
corporate earnings have continued to climb. With only three full years left
in the decade, one of the clearest manifestations of our Roaring 2020s

thesis has been what we call FEMO, or Fabulous Earnings Momentum.
FEMO is clearly visible in industry analysts' earnings expectations. They
currently expect S&P 500 operating earnings per share to rise to about
$364 per share in 2026 and $419 in 2027, implying growth of roughly 15%
in both years (chart). Toward the end of Q3 each year, we add another
year to our Earnings Squiggles framework. This year’s addition is 2028,
with earnings projected to rise another 17% that year to about $489 per

share.
For now, the analysts are more bullish than we are about earnings
prospects over the remainder of the decade. We are projecting $450 per
share in 2028, $475 in 2029, and $500 in 2030. If they are right, our S&P
500 target of 10,000 will be achieved well before the end of 2029!
The extended earnings boom is driven by strong revenue growth
expectations. Industry analysts expect S&P 500 revenues per share to

### Page 4

rise 12.7% in 2026, 9.2% in 2027, and another 8.3% in 2028 (chart).
Those expectations seem too exuberant to us.
Profit margins provide the other driver of the earnings story. Analysts
expect the S&P 500 profit margin to rise from 13.2% in 2025 to 15.7% this
year, 16.8% in 2027, and a record 18.1% in 2028 (chart). That is what we
would expect in our Roaring 2020s scenario, with stronger productivity
growth widening profit margins.
The earnings outlook is strong beyond the S&P 500 as well. Industry

analysts expect S&P 400 MidCap operating earnings per share to rise
roughly 19% in 2026, 14% in 2027, and 13% in 2028 (chart).

### Page 5

S&P 600 SmallCap earnings are expected to increase about 21% this
year, 16% in 2027, and 15% in 2028 (chart). That suggests FEMO is
broad-based.
II. Blue skies ahead for the economy?
(1) Consumer spending. The latest Redbook data provide yet another
sign of continued strength in consumer spending. Redbook same-store
sales rose 8.3% y/y during the week of October 2, well above the 5.8%
average for 2025 and underscoring the consumer’s continued resilience

(chart).

### Page 6

(2) Labor market. ADP estimates that private-sector payrolls rose 23,750
in the week through September 19, up from an upwardly revised 22,500
the prior week and marking the fifth consecutive acceleration in weekly job
gains (chart). It was also the strongest weekly increase in three months.
The ADP numbers for private industry payrolls make more sense to us
than the Bureau of Labor Statistics’ payroll employment data.
(3) Trade balance. The latest US trade data point to still strong US and

global economic growth. US imports rose 4.3% m/m and 23.6% y/y in
August, accelerating from July, while US exports increased 1.4% m/m and
12.3% y/y. Imports reached a $3.36 trillion annualized rate, versus $2.03
trillion for exports, leaving a merchandise trade deficit of $1.33 trillion
annualized (chart). The gap reflects exceptionally strong US demand for
imported goods.

### Page 7

Nominal and real US merchandise exports plus imports rose 23.1% y/y
and 7.1%, respectively, in August (chart). That suggests global trade
remains firmly in expansion territory and is consistent with strong global
economic growth.
Selected technology imports reached a record $56.6 billion in August
(chart). The AI buildout is highly import-intensive and requires large
volumes of semiconductors, servers, networking equipment, and other
hardware produced overseas.

### Page 8

clause.

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 9 月 30 日

1 群益投顧 
金像電 (2368 TT) 於 09/29/2026 董事會核准總金額 79 億 元 的 資 本 支 出 預 算 案 ， 預 計 自
4Q26 起 陸 續 進 行 投 資，並 決 議 洽 定 特 定 應 募 人 為Amazon.com, Inc(AMZN US) ，實 際
私募價格為 856 元 ， 預 計 此 次 私 募 規 模 為 15.89 億元、占金像電 2Q26 股本 0.36% 。
研 究 部 認 為，金 像 電 作 為 既 有 ASIC 伺 服 器 板 的 生 產 廠 商，隨 著 CSP 業者未來資本支
出 逐 步 放 大 、 對 算 力 的 要 求 也 直 接 提 升 PCB 板 規 格 與 產 品 種 類 ， 金 像 電 也 勢 必 增 加
資 本 支 出 以 維 持 產 業 競 爭 力 。 綜 合 以 上 ， 考 量 新 產 品 、 新 業 務 、 新 產 能 將 持 續 挹 注 金
像電 2027 年的營運，故維持金像電 Buy 的 投 資 建 議 ， 目 標 價 1,480 元 (22.70 PER X 
2027F EPS) 。

研 究 員 ： 吳 建 弘   
A51102@cim.capital.com.tw

金 像 電 09/29 /2026 董 事 會 核 准 資 本 支 出 案 與 公 告 第 一 次 私 募 案 對 象：  
金像電 (2368 TT) 於 09/29/2026 董 事 會 核 准 總 金 額 79 億 元 的 資 本 支 出 預 算
案 ， 預 計 自 4Q26 起 陸 續 進 行 投 資 ， 主 要 進 行 38 億 元 的 土 地 預 算 、41 億元
的 建 廠 與 設 備 資 本 支 出 。 
並決議 2026 年 第 一 次 私 募 普 通 股 定 價 及 應 募 人 資 訊 ，已 洽 定 特 定 應 募 人 為
Amazon.com, Inc(AMZN US) ， 預 計 本 次 私 募 增 資 認 購 股 數 為 1,856,308 股，
實 際 私 募 價 格為 856 元 。 此 次 私 募 規 模 為 15.89 億 元 、 占 金 像 電 2Q26 股本
0.36% 。  
研 究 部 評 析 
參考 04/16/2026 金 像 電 決 議 辦 理 私 募 增 資 案 的 內 容 ， 此 次Amazon 入股，
預 計 將 擴 大金 像 電 的 伺 服 器 業 務 範 圍 ， 提 高 營 運 績 效 。而 金 像 電 作 為既有
ASIC 伺 服 器 板 的 生 產 廠 商 ， 隨 著 CSP 業 者 未 來 資 本 支 出 逐 步 放 大 、 對 算
力 的 要 求 直 接 提 升 PCB 板 規 格 與 產 品 種 類 ， 金 像 電 也 勢 必 增 加 資 本 支 出 以
維 持 產 業 競 爭 力 。 
展望 2027 年 ， 隨 著 新世代的 AI ASIC 伺 服 器 產 品 逐 漸 量 產 ， 新 產 品 、 新
業務 、 新 產 能 將 持 續 挹 注金 像 電 營 運 ， 推 升 後 續 展 望 。 而 09/29/2026 的資
本 支 出 案 ， 研 究 部 基 於 設 備 交 期 規 劃 ， 預 計 將 投 入2028 年後 的 營運。  
綜 合 以 上 ， 考 量新 產 品 、 新 業 務 、 新 產 能 將 持 續 挹 注 金 像 電2027 年的營
運， 故 維 持 金 像 電 Buy 的 投 資 建 議 ， 目 標 價 1,480 元 (22.70 PER X 2027F 
EPS) 。

產業速報

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Gigabyte 
We are Neutral rated on Gigabyte with a 12-month target price of NT$487. Our TP is 
based on a 14.1x 2027E P/E which is based on PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E 
multiple correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s share 
performance. 
Key upside/downside risks include: 1) stronger-/weaker-than-expected PC market 
recovery, 2) faster-/slower-than-expected gaming PC growth, and 3) 
faster-/slower-than-expected AI server ramp-up.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 24 Sep 2026 close.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Pegatron (4938.TW): AI servers in expansion; New smartphone model 
with Broadband entering peak season to support growth ahead; Sell
28 September 2026 | 10:33AM HKT
3-month revenues preview: We expect sequential revenue growth in Sep-Dec, 
mainly driven by AI server racks ramp up with higher ASP and major smartphone 
brand customer’s new model launches (report link). Overall, we project Pegatron’s 
3Q/4Q26E revenues to grow +18%/+25% YoY, driven by: (1) new model launches 
from a major smartphone customer supporting September and 4Q26 revenue 
growth; (2) a continuing ramp-up in AI server shipments, across rack-level and 
baseboard-based, riding on the growing demand in Neocloud and enterprises, (3) 
broadband, IoT, and game console entering peak season. We are positive on 
Pegatron’s expansion from consumer electronics to AI servers and automotive 
electronics, while the high memory costs could cap PC and smartphone market 
growth. Maintain Sell on stretched valuation.

Pegatron’s Aug revenue was up 34% YoY / 3% MoM to NT$88bn, or 12% lower than 
our estimate. This reflects that high memory costs continue to cap PC and 
smartphone growth, despite a strong ramp-up in AI servers. Aug PC shipments was 
down 49% YoY to 0.40-0.45m, reflecting the impact of consumption pull in under 
the high memory costs. Its major smartphone brand customer launched new models 
in Sep, supporting Pegatron’s growth in Sep and 4Q26; prior to September, the 
company suffered from model transition (i.e. declining demand for older models 
while new models were in a slow shipment ramp-up phase).

Earnings revision: We factor in Pegatron’s Aug revenue and our 2026-28E net income 
largely unchanged. We cut our 2026-28E revenues by 1% / 1% / 1% to reflect the miss in 
consumer electronics under high memory costs. Our 2026-28E GM, OPM and NM are 
largely unchanged.

Valuation: We continue to derive our 12M TP based on near-term P/E, and our target 
P/E is updated to 10.8x 2027E EPS, (vs. 10.6x previously), derived from the refreshed 
peers correlation of forward year earnings growth and P/E.  With the updated earnings 
and target multiple, our 12M TP is revised up to NT$76.9 (vs. NT$75.5 previously). The 
target P/E of 10.8x is within the company’s historical avg. and avg.-1std forward trading 
P/E. Maintain Sell.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Pegatron 
We are Sell rated on Pegatron with a 12-month target price of NT$76.9. Our TP is based 
on a 10.8x 2027E P/E, which is derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple 
correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s share performance.  
Key risks: 1) stronger-than-expected smartphone market recovery, 2) 
faster-than-expected AI server ramp-up, and 3) stronger-than-expected demand on 
consumer electronics.

return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

## Document 076: 260929_ms_TDK-Taiyo-Yuden

### Page 1

M
Update
Yageo Corp.  | Asia Pacific
TDK-Taiyo Yuden MOU: high-
end wall rises; cycle thesis
intact
The MOU validates AI-driven high-cap MLCC tightness, which
supports pricing and our MLCC up-cycle view.
TDK and Taiyo Yuden (both covered by Shoji Sato) signed an MOU to explore an
alliance in advanced MLCCs/inductors –  including manufacturing/procurement
cooperation, potential METI subsidies under Japan's Economic Security Act, and a
possible future capital tie-up.

Our take: We estimate MLCC demand next year at more than 40bn units of 47uF+,
which crowds out  midrange/low-end MLCC capacity.
1. We believe  de-specing to ≤22uF MLCCs is not a viable workaround.
Substituting high-cap MLCCs with larger counts of lower-capacitance parts
requires broad real estate that simply does not exist on today's densely
packed GPU/accelerator boards.
2. Switching to tantalum-polymer capacitors merely trades one shortage for
another, given equally tight tantalum supply.

3. So the only realistic industry response, in our view, is reallocating midrange/
low-end MLCC capacity toward high-cap production. That would structurally
tightens midrange/low-end supply, underpinning broad-based pricing power.
Things to watch:
• Whether a capital alliance materializes,
• METI subsidy size (this determines how distorted the high-end cost curve
gets),
• Counter-move by Murata (also covered by Shoji Sato) counter-move, and
• Any sign the alliance extends into mid-cap products.

See our latest report on MLCC here: Global Technology: MLCC: Continued Demand
Growth for High-Value-added Products for AI & Data Centers (15 Sep 2026)

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

ChipMOS Technologies 29 September 2026 ab 2
Forecast returns

Forecast stock return 60.0%

Forecast excess return 53.8%
Company Description
ChipMOS is a semiconductor back-end supplier with 45-50% of its sales from display driver
IC (~40% from TV and 40% from smartphone and IT and 20% from automotive), 35-40%
from memory (~40% DRAM, ~40% from specialty flash and ~20% from mainstream NAND)

and 10-15% from logic (TV SoC) and mixed signal IC (MEMS, PMIC).
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We have a Buy rating and a NT$166 price target, based on 23x average 2027-28E PE (vs. its
normalized 5-27x PE range). Our target multiple is in line with the average of memory and
driver IC OSAT peers.Downside risks include a weaker-than-expected tech end-market
demand recovery, growing China competition, failure to diversify into the SiPh/optical

component bumping business and a slower ramp of its logic testing business.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

3Q26 營收 646.92 億元(+40.9%QoQ)。獲利方面，儘管中東衝突推升

運價將緩步回落；美線旺季需求亦近尾聲，惟受巴拿馬運河通行量下

陽明為全球貨櫃運力第 9 大航
商，市占率 2.2% 
 陽明成立於 1972 年 12 月 ， 實收資本額349.21 億元 。 截至1H26， 全球共6,407
位員工及 233 個代理行 ， 每週提供105 條定期航線服務 。 根據Alphaliner 統計 ，
截至 Sep-26，陽明為全球貨櫃運力第 9 大航商，市佔率約 2.2%。自 Feb-25
起，陽明所屬聯盟由原先 THE Alliance 改組為 Premier Allianc e。陽明 1H26
航線運費收入占比別：亞洲近洋線 13%，亞二線(含中東、南美、紅海、澳洲、
南亞) 12%，歐洲線(含西北歐、地中海、越大西洋、歐地) 39%，美洲線(含美
東、美西) 36%。 
以船隊規模而言，截至 3Q26 陽明共有 99 艘船，包含自有船共 65 艘，租入船
34 艘，總運力 77.3 萬 TEU。為持續追求汰舊換新計畫，陽明預計 2026 年將
交付 5 艘 1.5 萬 TEU LNG 雙燃料船。

陽明 Aug-26 營收 216.11 億元(+3.72%MoM，+56.17%Y oY，2026 年累計營
收+11.93%Y oY)，月增主因受惠歐美線價量齊揚。元富預估陽明 3Q26 營收
646.92 億元(+40.9%QoQ，+53.7%Y oY)。獲利方面，儘管中東衝突推升燃油
成本，且貨量成長帶動變動成本同步增加，惟 3Q26 運價仍維持高檔，故上修
毛利率至 31.54%、營益率 25.94%。假設匯率持穩，預估稅後淨利 156.37 億
元(+172.7%QoQ，+158.5%Y oY)，預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 4.48 元。

Exhibit 9：近年新加坡海運燃油價格

Sources: Bloomberg(Sep-26)， Masterlink

2026 年供需缺口相對收斂， 預估
2026 年稅後 EPS 8.17 元 
 根據 Alphaliner Aug-26 預測，2026 年貨櫃供給年增 4.5%，需求年增 2.5%，
供給增幅仍高於需求，惟供需缺口較 2025 年逐步收斂。短期供給方面，目前
歐美港口以局部壅塞為主，如：9/23 歐洲鹿特丹港等待天數約 3 天、美國長灘
港約 6 天；中東地區則因地緣政治風險仍在，遠東-波斯灣航線運力供給受限，
支撐運價維持高檔；目前部分貨物改由印度西岸轉運後以陸路運往中東，使得
印度西岸港口壅塞，等待天數約 3-8 天。

展望 4Q26，需求面而言，歐線隨傳統旺季結束，終端需求動能轉弱，預期運
價將緩步回落；美線旺季需求亦近尾聲，惟受巴拿馬運河通行量下降及中國十
一長假前提前出貨支撐，預期短期運價仍維持高原期。另，9/24 川習會後關稅
政策變化仍須持續關注。供給面而言，美伊衝突及紅海通行風險仍存，儘管
Gemini 聯盟逐步恢復部分紅海航線，其餘航商短期跟進可能性仍低 ，繞行好
望角仍將限制有效運力供給。

整體而言，預估 2026 年營收 2,003.1 億元(+22.5%Y oY)，考量近期中東衝突
再度升溫，且歐美航線貨量占比提升帶動燃油成本增加，預估稅後淨利 285.1
億元(+66.8%Y oY)，2026 年稅後 EPS 8.17 元。展望 2027 年，考量新船陸續
交付 ， 供給壓力逐步增加 ， 且假設無新增關稅政策干擾 ， 需求回歸傳統季節性，
預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 3.44 元。

Telecom & Networking Equipment  | North America
Potential FCC Rules on Optical
Transceivers More Likely to
Come in at 3.2T
Recent DC meetings highlight 3.2T as the most likely generation
to see restrictions on China-based optical transceivers. This
echoes conversations with LITE during the Q noting that any
restrictions would need to be phased in. See potential rules
keeping laser market tight, a pos for LITE/COHR.
Morgan Stanley & Co. LLC

Morgan Stanley MUFG Securities Co., Ltd.+
Yu Shirakawa
Equity Analyst
Yu.Shirakawa@morganstanleymufg.com                                                +81 3 6836-5432
Telecom & Networking Equipment
North America
Industry View In-Line
What Happened: Morgan Stanley strategists recently  met with a senior Washington
telecom, FCC and export-control legal team with direct engagement across the
White House and FCC. Discussion focused on the Administration’s efforts to reduce

reliance on Chinese technology, with the FCC Covered List emerging as the more
likely near-term mechanism for optical transceivers (a note we made in our AI Safety
DC trip wrap-up last week). Policy discussions suggest 3.2T transceivers would likely
be where restrictions would be phased in, potentially as soon as October, with
industry recently asked to demonstrate credible U.S. or friend-shoring plans before
3.2T becomes broadly deployed. Our conversations noted that discussions are also

indicating Chinese-built transceivers could still be importable, even at 3.2T, as long
as 65% of the BOM comes from US-based companies (DSP / laser would get you
pretty close). Initial 3.2T volumes are not expected to ramp in volume until 2029,
limiting the near-term revenue impact of a restriction even if action comes sooner.
We would see these rules as having the potential to avoid creating a constraint
on hyperscalers' ability to build today, but keeping the  laser market tighter given

laser would largely need to be from a US-based vendor to meet a 65% value
threshold, a positive for LITE/COHR.
How this echoes conversation with LITE earlier in the quarter: We hosted LITE at
Nasdaq Asia in late August, where we asked the company about potential FCC rules.
At the time they noted that the FCC had been in contact with them about potential
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Recent DC trip by our strategist   team gave a greater idea of what potential FCC
rules on optical transceivers could look like.
Potential restrictions, likely to come in at 3.2T, limit Chinese-based transceivers,
with a potential workaround if US-based content above 65% of the BOM.
Solution seemingly a win-win for LITE/COHR, with laser market likely to stay

tight, but not having to take on lower module assembly business.
Also, does not create bottlenecks for hyperscalers.
Assuring InP substrate delivery will be key to look at going forward.
October 1, 2026 04:09 AM GMT

### Page 2

M
Update
2
rules, with the company noting their feedback was that the rules would need to be
phased in over time to prevent material supply/demand dislocation in the market
with 60-65% of transceivers coming from China-based transceiver vendors. They
also noted that pure transceiver volumes alone, at current pricing structures, would
not be the most attractive business for them to bring on. These rules, which are
both phased in and don't limit Chinese assemblers, as long as enough content is

US-based, would seemingly address both of these concerns.
Our Thoughts:
1. 3.2T looks like the likely policy pressure point, but the near-term impact
should be limited - Administration efforts appear focused on reshaping the
supply chain before 3.2T reaches volume rather than disrupting the existing
800G and 1.6T base. With the 3.2T ramp not expected to begin until 2028
and broader adoption closer to 2029, an October Covered List action would
provide roughly two years before meaningful volumes enter the market.

Hyperscalers are already working on plans intended to demonstrate greater
U.S. or friend-shored manufacturing, suggesting the policy objective is
increasingly about determining where the next generation is built rather
than immediately displacing current Chinese supply.
2. Laser pricing looks like the stronger read-through versus transceiver
share - A Covered List framework does not necessarily require Chinese
module vendors to lose significant share if they can increase the U.S.

content of the underlying BOM. Combining a U.S. DSP from Marvell with
laser content from LITE/COHR could potentially move a Chinese-built
transceiver to the 65% U.S.-value threshold discussed in our strategists
team policy meeting. Such an outcome would leave Innolight and Eoptolink
(both covered by Andy Meng) with less flexibility to substitute toward
China-based lasers while allowing them to remain relevant at the module
level, reducing the risk from China-based laser makers. We therefore see a

stronger read-through for high-speed laser pricing and LITE/COHR
component share than for a wholesale redistribution of transceiver share.
3. Replacement capacity not ready; nor is it the most attractive opportunity
- As noted above, LITE noted that assembly of transceivers (mid-30's to low-
40s gross margin product), is not the most attractive opportunity for US-
based optical companies (vs. EMLs and lasers well north of 50% steady
state and approaching 75% today). While 3.2T is not yet in production, we

use AAOI's current 800G and 1.6T capacity ramp as an illustration of the
scale of US supply available to absorb a potential future demand shift. AAOI
(not covered), the only material US-based production of transceivers, is
currently ramping capacity. AAOI exited Q2 with manufacturing capacity
approaching 200,000 800G and 1.6T units per month and expects to reach
~650,000 units per month by year-end 2026, while management continues
to expect demand to outpace production through mid-2027. AAOI’s YE26

capacity annualizes to ~12M 800G/1.6T units, versus ~101M units
combined for Innolight and Eoptolink based on annualized 1H capacity
(44.3M and 56.7M, respectively), or >8x AAOI. Based on AAOI’s year-end
capacity plans and our Internet team’s hyperscaler capex work, we estimate
AAOI could support less than 4 GW of hyperscaler capacity even before

### Page 3

M
Update
Morgan Stanley Research 3
factoring in manufacturing utilization. Replacing a meaningful portion of
Innolight and Eoptolink’s supply would therefore require a substantial multi-
year capacity build, with limited ability for existing U.S. suppliers to absorb
those volumes in the near term.
4. InP supply and sourcing outside of China will still be areas to watch -
Potential FCC rules could create a win-win for LITE/COHR by reducing the
risk of China-based laser makers flooding the market, without forcing them

to build significant transceiver module capacity to absorb lower-margin
assembly business. Were China to view the rules as too one-sided, it could
respond with tighter restrictions on InP substrate exports, a key bottleneck
to laser capacity ramping. At the same time, sourcing outside of China could
broaden the beneficiary set beyond U.S. suppliers, supporting InP substrate
demand at JX Advanced Metals (5016 JP), while providing a tailwind for
Sumitomo Electric’s (5802 JP) CWLD products and Furukawa Electric’s

(5801 JP) DFB lasers.

We think the capex budget might gradually shift to AI compute
and AI networking in 2028, as memory usage needs to be
optimized. We see positives for TSMC and UMC.
TSMC 3Q26 earnings preview: We expect the company to raise full-year revenue
guidance to the low-to-mid-40% range (vs.  above 40%) Y/Y, implying 4Q26 revenue

will rise 10%-15% Q/Q amid very strong 3nm demand. 3Q26 gross margin should
reach the high end of guidance of 67.5% However, should Intel focus on internal
capacity expansion, we think TSMC would maintain 2nm capacity expansion at the
same pace and its capex at US$54bn for 2026. For 2027, we see TSMC’s capex
reaching US$75bn to US$80bn.
In terms of 2027 wafer price hikes, we see leading edge (7nm and below) up 5%-10%
and mature nodes up 0%-3%; hence, gross margin could approach 70% in 2027. As a

result, we raise  our TSMC price target  by 3% to NT$3,088.
Key topics for upcoming TSMC 3Q26 earnings call on October 15:
1. Would TSMC consider working with TereFab  (Taiwan News, Oct 3 2026) and
building new fab sites in Texas (Bloomberg, Oct 1 2026)? Our view is that expansion
in Texas could be necessary, considering customer demand and limited space at the
Arizona fab in five years.
2. If Intel were to increase capex, would that reduce outsourcing at TSMC? Our

recent industry checks suggest some trimming of Intel’s 3nm and 2nm; how might
TSMC reallocate its resources?
3. How would TSMC address large reticle size chip demand? Our checks suggest
MediaTek handled TPU v10 IceFish reticle size initially at 14x; could TSMC’s CoWoS
or CoPoS be able to support such packaging?
We raise  our UMC price target by 36% to NT$188 on AI networking (SiPh)
business upside: Some investors remain on the sidelines amid ongoing smartphone

and PC demand weakness. We have seen some order cuts in PC semis recently, as
some consumer analog IC customers (e.g., Silergy) have not been able to pass
through UMC’s wafer price hike. Therefore, some investors do not expect any UMC’s
price hike in 2027. However, two growth drivers could help UMC raise wafer price
for 8-inch in 2027: 1) Samsung DDI outsourcing, as we noted in our earlier  Foundry
Floorplan, and 2) growing SiPh PIC demand, in particular after UMC introduced its

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

shipments. If so, TSMC should retain strong pricing power and could increase leading-edge
wafer prices by 5%–10% in 2027, reflecting both the value it delivers to customers and the
potential for margin expansion.

TSMC 2nm and 3nm capacity growth remains robust; 5nm
set to decline
Consistent with our demand forecast, TSMC indicated very strong capacity requirements
for both 2nm and 3nm starting in 2026, implying a 70% capacity CAGR for N2 from 2026

to 2028. TSMC also suggested that 5nm capacity will begin to decline from 2027, in line
with our forecast that 5nm demand will drop as Blackwell transitions to Rubin on 3nm.
Separately, advanced packaging capacity expansion should remain in focus for TSMC in
2027. This includes, but is not limited to, CoWoS and SoIC, for which we now expect
capacity to reach 200kwpm and 40kwpm, respectively, by 2027.

How might TSMC address Intel's competition in both wafer
foundry and advanced packaging?
Intel EMIB-T remains competitive in 2029; how TSMC will
address?
With demand in AI continuing to boom, we expect TSMC and its OSAT partners to
expedite capacity expansion plans for CoWoS. We see TSMC's CoWoS capacity building
further to 260kwpm in 2028, from 130/200kwpm in 2026/2027, with 2028's expansion
mainly focused in Arizona's AP9/10 and potentially in AP7. TSMC is now conducting trial

production for CoPoS in Taoyuan. If the progress goes well, we would expect TSMC to
start building its CoPoS capacity, with location also likely to be in AP9/10 or AP7P3.
We expect the non-TSMC camp to expand CoWoS capacity to 110kwpm by 2028 year-end,
from 80kwpm by end-2027. For ASE/SPIL, we expect FoCoS+CoWoS capacity to rise from
30kwpm at 2027-end to 50-60kwpm at 2028-end, with expansion focused on CoWoS-L
and CoWoS-R. For Amkor, we expect CoWoS capacity to increase from 30kwpm at 2027-

end to 55-60kwpm at 2028-end, also focused on CoWoS-L and CoWoS-R.

What about  Intel EMIB capacity build?
For Intel, we see current average EMIB-M capacity of 110kwpm falling slightly to 95kwpm
in 2027 as it converts some toward EMIB-T. We see the major customers of EMIB-M being
Trainium3, upcoming Trainium 4 and its in-house server CPU. Average EMIB-T capacity is

around 5kwpm in 2026, which we expect to further scale to 15-20kwpm in 2027 and 40-
45kwpm in 2028. All of the capacity mentioned above is converted for  12-inch wafer
equivalent, which we believe would be much more easier to communicate with compared
to TSMC CoWoS capacity.

consuming most of the capacity during 2027-28. We maintain our view that Intel EMIB-T

Indeed, we have seen some TSMC customers surveying (testing vehicles) Intel EMIB-T due
to the design for larger chip size, lower cost, and intention to seek a second source
because TSMC's CoWoS capacity is still relatively tight. However, we believe TSMC could
still try to expand its packaging size by leveraging different technologies to meet
customers' needs. We think it remains concerned about whether it is going to open its
front-end computing die production/know-how to its competitor, Intel, for back-end

packaging.
Our prediction of the trend after 2028 –  TSMC might:
1. Pull in CoPoS (panel-level process) with both 300mm and 500mm square panel
size
2. Introduce 3.5D packaging with SoIC for vertical chip stacking (Meta MTIA is
surveying)
3. Adopt glass core substrate to solve warpage for >9.7x reticle-size chips

Source: Winway, TSMC
Pros and cons of TSMC's CoWoS, Intel's EMIB, TSMC's
CoPoS, and SoIC
TSMC’s CoWoS, Intel’s EMIB, TSMC’s CoPoS, and TSMC’s SoIC represent different points
along the advanced packaging spectrum, each optimizing for a different balance of
bandwidth, cost, scalability and integration complexity. We think future design of the
chips is likely to combine these approaches—for example, using CoWoS for lateral

integration and SoIC for vertical stacking—to achieve optimal system-level performance.
Exhibit 12: Comparison between TSMC's CoWoS, CoPoS, SoIC, and Intel's EMIB-T
CoWoS CoPoS SoIC EMIB
2.5D 2.5D 3D 2.5D
Wafer level Panel level Chip/die stacking Substrate level

Efficiency for large chip (use of the subrate area) Low High NA High

Cost at 9.7x retical size (including yield issue) High Theoretical low Highest Theoret ical low
Technology maturity (opposite of execution risk) High Low Medium Low
Thermal challenge Medium Theoretical low Highest Theoretical low

maturing

In summary,

### Page 9

Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 9
• CoWoS excels in high-bandwidth 2.5D integration but is costly and capacity-limited
• EMIB offers a more cost-effective and modular alternative with some performance
trade-offs
• CoPoS targets large-scale, cost-efficient integration but is still maturing
• SoIC delivers the highest performance through 3D stacking at the expense of
complexity and thermal constraints.
Exhibit 13: CoWoS, CoPoS and FoPLP comparison
Source: Yole

Exhibit 14: Intel's EMIB should easily support larger chips  with more reticles  (>12) if its
supply chain can execute well
Source: Intel

### Page 10

M
Idea
10
UMC is well positioned for Silicon Photonic IC (PIC) foundry
In our previous Foundry Floorplan, we presented a constructive view on UMC given its AI-
related revenue. Management indicated AI revenue of US$300mn this year (3-5% of
revenue), potentially more than tripling to US$1bn in three years (2029).
UMC has a  capacity expansion plan to capture AI demand, including a clean room for
advanced packaging for AI chips, such as wafer-on-wafer and memory-on-memory stacking.

Some investors remain on the sidelines amid ongoing smartphone and PC demand
weakness, and we have seen some order cuts of PC semis recently. Some think a UMC
price hike in 2027 wouldn’t go through, but we see two growth drivers helping it
successfully  hike wafer price for 8-inch in 2027: 1) Samsung DDI outsourcing, and 2)
growing SiPh PIC demand, in particular after UMC introduced its 400Gb per lane solution
(PIC + TFLN) solution.
How UMC is differentiated in PIC foundry

According to UMC, InP and TFLN are the two most promising solutions, as they are much
more mature than the other contenders. InP is facing severe shortage, adding to TFLN’s
momentum.
UMC Group’s TFLN program started in 2021 with 6” in mass production and 8” in
sampling. TFLN's   strengths include low loss, high bandwidth (> 110 GHz), good thermal
stability, and strong linearity.
Integrating TFLN with PIC is a challenge as well as an opportunity. Advanced packaging

schemes and micro transfer printing are both seen as promising candidates. UMC is
developing the integration technology, leveraging its expertise in PIC, TFLN, and advanced
packaging.
Adoption of higher-performance modulators (TFLN/InP) and improvement in photo diodes
are key factors affecting deployment of plain-vanilla (non-WDM, IM/DD) DCIs with 400
Gbps/channel performance and beyond, which is very close to TSMC's mainstream PIC
technology Micro Ring.

Exhibit 15: UMC differentiates in TFLN process offering for PIC
Source: UMC

About Foundry Floorplan
In 2011,  we first published this product (Asia/Pacific Foundry: The Foundry Floorplan) to
provide our analysis of foundry industry dynamics. We have resumed this practice as
global foundry competition remains active. Foundry supply is also a critical resource for
future AI chips, whether in the global market or China's local market.
Below are prior reports from this series:
• The Foundry Floorplan: All boats riding on the same tide – mature node up-cycle

begins (19 May 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: TSMC preview update; CoWoS vs. EMIB competition (14
Apr 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: Raise TSMC's 2027e and 2028e Capex (9 Mar 2026)
• The Foundry Floorplan: Update on TSMC's 2027 capex; interposer outsourcing to
Vanguard? (13 Jan 2026)
• Asia/Pacific Foundry: The Foundry Floorplan, April 2011

TSMC: Estimate and price target revisions
Our earnings forecasts increase 4% for 2026,  5% for  2027 and 3% for 2028: We raise
our 2026 revenue forecast and modestly increase our margin assumptions to reflect
stronger-than-expected 3Q26 performance, driven by robust AI demand, including
sustained Blackwell shipments. We raise our 2027–28 revenue assumptions by 3%–5%,
reflecting the increase in strong leading-edge demand as well as the wafer price hike.

Our PT increases to NT$3,088 from   NT$2,988, driven primarily by our estimate

revisions. Our PT implies a 20x P/E multiple on our new 2027 EPS forecast. TSMC
currently trades at 17x that estimate, close to its average NTM P/E of 16.5x since 2018. We
find the stock attractive at current levels.
We continue to derive our 12-month price target (base-case scenario) using a residual
income valuation framework. Our key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of
equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of 2.0%), an

intermediate growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4%.
Our bull and bear case scenario values increase to NT$3,720 (from NT$3,600) and NT
$1,700 (from NT$1,645), respectively.

We raise our price target to NT$188 from NT$138: We continue to derive our price
target (base case scenario value) from a residual income model, as we believe it best
represents the value of the company. Besides our estimate increases, we raise our
intermediate growth rate from 12.5% to 13.5% due to structural changes in the industry's
supply/demand dynamics. Other key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of

MS Rating
▪ We are OW as we see structural changes
in the mature node industry's
supply/demand dynamics.
▪ Its foundry quality is comparable to
TSMC's in mature nodes. Along with

specialty technologies, this could help offset
part of the margin downside.
▪ Non-AI market recovery remains uncertain
in 2027, yet UMC should still beneﬁt from
strong AI-related demand spillover from its
foundry peers.
▪ 12nm FinFET development is strategic. It
should be one of UMC's long-term
strengths, notably when Chinese foundries
cannot effectively expand FinFET capacity.
▪ The stock now trades at 4.1x 2027e BVPS.
That is well above its historical average

since 2009, but our price target implies 5.1x.

NT$270.00
7.5x 2027e BVPS
Stabilized semi demand; better-than-
expected 8-inch and trailing-edge 12-inch
demand: 1) Global semi sales grow >15% Y/Y

in 2026, and ex-TSMC foundry industry
grows >10% Y/Y; 2) UMC gains share in both
8- and 12-inch wafers thanks to technology
differentiation; 3) UMC receives more
outsourcing from customers, which helps ﬁll
22/28nm capacity and lifts blended wafer
ASP; 4) traction increases for UMC's
12nm/14nm FinFET technologies.
NT$188.00
5.2x 2027e BVPS
Healthy semi growth; improving demand for
8-inch and trailing-edge 12-inch: 1) Global
semi sales grow ~15% in 2026 and ex-TSMC

foundry industry grows 3-5% Y/Y; 2) 8-inch
utilization rate is above 75% with steady 12-
inch demand amid semi inventory digestion;
3) UMC hikes wafer prices slightly in
response to strong AI-related demand.
NT$95.00
2.6x 2027e BVPS
Signiﬁcant semi industry decline; 8-inch and
trailing-edge 12-inch face weakening
demand: 1) Global semi sales grow only <5%
in 2026; 2) display semi and power semi
demand deteriorates further; 3) signiﬁcant
wafer pricing erosion given low fab

utilization and mature node peers'
competition; 4) depreciation ramp-up of
more than 30% Y/Y in 2026 without
sufﬁcient wafer order inﬂow to sustain
UMC's gross margin.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

ab 5 October 2026
Global Research
Global I/O
Smartphones
August ’26 Sell-Through: No signs of recovery
yet
Global smartphone unit sell-through -8.9% YoY August '26 YTD
Global smartphone unit sell-through (source: Counterpoint) for the month of August
'26 came in at 85.9m units (-0.1% MoM, -12.3% YoY), marking the ninth consecutive
month of YoY sell-through decline (was -12.2%/-10.9% YoY in July/June). By region, all
regions except for the US (-3.4% YoY) saw double-digit YoY declines, with India seeing

the most incremental downside at -17.7% YoY, followed by China (-17.3%), Europe (-
10.7%) and RoW (-10.1%). Following eight months of significant YoY weakness,
August '26 YTD global smartphone unit sell-through now sits at -8.9% YoY with all
regions well in negative comps (India/China/Europe/RoW/US: -12.3%/-10.5%/-8.7%/-
8.0%/-4.4% YoY).
Apple outperformance continues, Samsung beginning to rebound
Apple: YTD iPhone unit sell-through (149.3m units, +8.7% YoY) continues to highlight

the material outperformance of Apple, which remains the only major OEM (>10% unit
market share) in positive comps. With key iPhone suppliers optimistic on unit growth in
'27 (link) on the back of (1) the change in launch schedule (3 models each in 1Q27 and
3Q27) and (2) the introduction of iPhone Duo (~10m units in first 12 months), we
continue to forecast Apple strength to continue beyond '26 (UBSe sell-in: +8.5% YoY)
and into '27 (+4.7% YoY). Samsung: August '26 sell-through came in at 20.3m units

(+0.9% YoY), returning to positive comps for the first time since March '26, primarily on
the back of the release of the new flagship foldable models (Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, Fold8,
Flip8) released on August 7th. New foldable sales in August came in at 2.8m units, only
0.3m units (or 7.5%) below July+August '25 sales for last year's lineup (Galaxy Z Fold7,
Flip7, Flip7 FE; released on July 25 th, 2025) despite two less weeks of sales. Whilst
YTD sell-through still remains soft at -4.1% YoY, we believe resilient sales of the new

flagship model as well as incremental share gains in the mid-range should help Samsung
achieve YoY unit growth (UBSe '26E sell-in: +4.4% YoY). China Android: We continue
to see memory-driven downside materialising with Transsion, Oppo, Vivo, and Xiaomi
YoY sell-through decline now at -23.1%/-19.0%/-18.2%/-18.1%.
Reiterating our sell-in forecasts of -10.0%/-3.0% YoY in '26E/'27E
We entered the year with a cautious view on the smartphone industry (link) primarily on

the back of rapidly rising memory costs. We also flagged that these dynamics will be
much more penalising for Chinese OEMs focusing on the low-end/mid-range segment.
With the DRAM/NAND upcycles likely to extend until at least 2Q28E/4Q27E and unit
sell-through now down 8.9% YoY globally (and on average 16.0% for Transsion, Oppo,
Vivo, and Xiaomi) eight months into the year, we expect to see double-digit YoY unit
decline % and reiterate our '26E sell-in forecast of -10.0% YoY. Heading into '27, with

memory likely to remain an incremental headwind, we expect to see muted demand for
smartphones and see units declining further from a low base. We recently lowered our
YoY unit sell-in forecasts for '27E to -3.0% from +2.0% (link).
Selective on smartphone plays; prefer memory/MLCC suppliers
Within the smartphone supply chain, we continue to prefer MediaTek and Samsung
(both APAC Key Call Buy) as well as ASE, Hon Hai, JCET, Micron, Murata, SEMCO, SK

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
The handset industry, as part of consumer electronics, is cyclically sensitive to the economy
and so upside and downside risks relate to macro drivers. Other downside risks include the
rapid loss of market share due to poor product timing, design or distribution. The supply
chain companies into the handset industry also operate in a highly cyclical market both from
macro concerns and inventory cycles within the supply chain. These companies also operate

in a highly competitive environment with product innovation and R&D investments critical to
success. Valuation methods across the sector include multiple analysis.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

個股報告 
嘉晶(3016) 
8 吋 Si Epi 滿載支撐獲利，Ge/Si 擴產加速 
投資評等 目標價 
區間操作 $152 
前次投資建議：2026.04.30 
「區間操作」 ，目標價: $92

報告日期：2026.09.23 研 究 員：李彝安

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 嘉晶 26Q2 毛利率 23.5%優於預期，8吋 Si Epi 維持滿載，Ge/Si需求能見度延伸至 2028 年，
上修 2026/27F EPS 至 2.56/3.69，目標價 152 元(6.5x2027(F)PBR)，潛在上漲空間 14.7%。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/09/22 收盤價： 132.50 ➢ 26Q2 營收 13.09 億元，季增 23.6%、年增 37.1%，毛
利率 23.5%、營益率16.9%，稅後淨利1.88 億元、EPS 
0.65 元。營收僅略高於研究部原估12.92 億元，毛利率
較原估 18.4%高出 5.1ppt，主要反映8吋產品占比提高
及稼動率改善，因此上修獲利。 
➢ 26H1 8 吋 Si Epi 產能滿載，8吋占 Si Epi 營收比重由
去年同期 59%提升至 71%，MOSFET 與 PMIC 晶圓產
出年增 50%。既有產能仍無法滿足客戶需求，2026 年
新增設備預計自 2027 年陸續開出。 
➢ Compound Epi 營運改善，26H1 GaN/SiC營收分別年
增 20%/28%；6 吋 GaN 已轉虧為盈且產能滿載，SiC
則受惠日本 IDM 客戶自 26Q2 開始放量，稼動率提高
後虧損明顯收斂。 Ge/Si PD Epi 26Q2 營收季增
113%，26H2 營收預估較 26H1 成長 2.5 倍，2027 年
產能預計增至目前 3.5～4倍，訂單能見度延伸至2028
年；惟 26H1 營收占比僅約 1%，研究部推估 2027 年
占比仍約 4%～5%，短期 EPS貢獻有限。 
➢ 研究部上修 2026 年營收至 53.3 億元、毛利率 23.1%、
EPS 2.56元；2027 年營收 68.7 億元、毛利率25.1%、
EPS 3.69元 。 考量8吋 Si Epi維持滿載 、Compound Epi
獲利改善及 Ge/Si後續成長，給予 6.5x 27F PBR，目
標價 152 元。6.5x 已位於公司過去約 2–8x PBR 區間
偏高位置，反映目前獲利改善及成長能見度提升，評價
進一步上修空間有限；潛在上漲空間 14.7%，維持「區
間操作」評等。

營 運 分 析 
嘉晶為國內少數同時跨足矽磊晶、化合物半導體與矽光子之專業磊晶代工廠 
嘉晶（3016）成立於 1996 年，總部位於新竹科學園區，為國內專業半導體
磊晶 （Epitaxy） 代工廠，主要股東為漢民科技集團。公司早期以矽磊晶 （Silicon 
Epi）代工起家，產品應用於電源管理 IC（PMIC）、MOSFET 等功率半導
體領域；中期切入化合物半導體磊晶，建置氮化鎵（GaN）與碳化矽（SiC）
產線，跨入射頻與功率元件市場，下游應用涵蓋資料中心電源、電動車、再
生能源、人形機器人與航太科技；近期則進一步切入矽光子（ Silicon 
Photonics）用 Ge on Si（鍺在矽上）光偵測器（PD）磊晶代工，為國內少
數同時具備三大磊晶平台量產能力之專業業者。 
公司於台灣設有 4 吋、5 吋、6 吋、8 吋多尺寸磊晶產線，磊晶製程涵蓋  
Silicon、GaN、SiC、Ge on Si 等多種材料系統，具備磊晶配方設計、設備
運轉、品質與良率管控之完整 Know-How。客戶結構上，下游涵蓋 Fabless 
設計業者與 IDM 廠，訂單來源以台灣為主，日本與其他地區次之。

圖一、嘉晶(3016) 2026H1產品別營收比重

個股報告 
26Q2 毛利率優於預期，上修 2026年獲利 
嘉晶 26Q2 營收 13.09 億元，季增 23.6%、年增 37.1%，毛利率 23.5%，季
增 5.7ppt，營益率 16.9%，稅後淨利 1.88 億元、EPS 0.65 元；26H1 營收
23.68億元 、 年增27.9%， 毛利率20.9%，EPS 1.00元 ， 較去年同期EPS -0.14
元明顯改善。 
26Q2營收與研究部原估 12.92億元差異有限 ， 惟毛利率23.5%較原估 18.4%
高出 5.1ppt，獲利優於預期主要來自產品組合及稼動率改善。26H1 持續將
小尺寸產能轉往 8 吋，8 吋產品占比提高，加上 8 吋產線維持滿載，帶動固
定成本吸收及整體獲利改善。26Q2 毛利率尚未反映漲價效益，價格調整自
26Q3 才開始，26H2 將陸續反映效益。  
研究部將 2026 年營收由前次 51.0 億元上修至 53.3 億元，年增 37.0%；毛
利率由 18.1%上修至 23.1%，稅後淨利 7.6億元、EPS由 1.70 元上修至 2.56
元。預估 26Q3/26Q4 營收 14.59/15.06 億元，季增 11.5%/3.2%，毛利率
24.6%/24.9%，EPS均為 0.8元。

8吋 Si Epi維持滿載，2027年新增產能陸續開出 
Si Epi 仍為嘉晶主要營收與獲利來源，應用涵蓋 PMIC、Power MOSFET、
IGBT、Driver 及 Protection IC 等功率元件。AI 伺服器功率密度提升，帶動
PMIC與 Power MOSFET 需求增加，26H1 8吋 Si Epi產能滿載，營收占 Si 
Epi比重由去年同期 59%提升至 71%，MOSFET與 PMIC晶圓產出年增50%。 
國內部分晶圓廠原先具備自有 Epi產能，但AI 相關 PMIC需求增加後，既有
產能不足，帶動委外需求增加。26H2 Si Epi 營收預估年增約 42%，8 吋既
有產能滿載後仍無法滿足客戶需求，目前客戶持續要求擴產，2027 年新增產
能開出後仍為主要營收成長來源。 
AI伺服器功率需求亦帶動不同材料平台成長，其中Si Epi主要對應 PMIC 與
MOSFET；800V高壓直流架構則增加 GaN及 SiC等化合物半導體的應用機
會 。 三項產品雖均受惠AI電源需求 ， 但目前對嘉晶獲利的貢獻仍以8吋 Si Epi
為主。

26Q3 開始調價，26H2營收與獲利優於 26H1 
原物料及相關成本上升，自 26Q3 開始針對不同產品與客戶調整價格。由於
價格調整自 26Q3 才開始 ，26Q2 毛利率由 17.8%提升至 23.5%主要仍反映 8
吋占比及產能利用率改善，而非 ASP上升。26H2 Si Epi 需求維持高檔，加
上價格調整開始反映，研究部預估26Q3/26Q4 毛利率分別為 24.6%/24.9%，
高於 26Q2 的 23.5%。26H2 營收 29.65 億元，較 26H1 成長 25.2%，2026
全年營收 53.3 億元、年增 37.0%，符合公司全年營收年增>35%的展望。 
2027 年毛利率預估進一步提升至 25.1%，主要反映產品組合持續改善、
Compound Epi 獲利回升，以及 Ge/Si較高毛利產品占比增加；另一方面，
2026 年新增設備的折舊將自 2027～28年陸續認列，部分抵銷產品組合改善
效益，因此並未假設 Si Epi毛利率持續大幅上升。

圖二、嘉晶(3016)各季營收及獲利走勢預估

資料來源：Cmoney；兆豐投顧預估

Compound Epi營運改善，GaN轉盈、SiC虧損收斂 
26H1 Compound Epi 營運明顯改善，GaN營收年增 20%、SiC年增 28%。
GaN 自 25Q3 至 26Q2 連續四季成長，6 吋產能目前已滿載並由虧轉盈，8
吋則開始小量生產；現有 6 吋需求高於可供產能，客戶有對公司提出擴產需
求。GaN 後續擴產狀況須配合客戶產品平台及下游 Foundry 進度。目前 6
吋訂單充足，但長期產業逐步往 8吋發展，因此新增產能將依客戶與Foundry
合作成熟度調整。SiC 方面，日本 IDM客戶自 26Q2 開始放量，帶動產能利
用率回升，原先投資虧損明顯縮小。中國SiC市場仍面臨較激烈的價格競爭，
目前嘉晶業務以日本及其他非中國市場為主。

26H2 Compound Epi 營收預估年增 86%，改善幅度高於 Si Epi。惟現階段
Compound Epi營收規模仍低於 Si Epi，2026～27年對獲利的主要貢獻在於
GaN由虧轉盈及 SiC虧損持續收斂，而非取代 Si Epi成為主要獲利來源。

Compound Epi 營運改善，GaN轉盈、SiC虧損收斂 
Ge/Si PD Epi主要應用於高速光通訊接收端 Photodiode，目前對應800G 及
1.6T 可插拔式光模組。26Q2 營收季增 113%，26H2 營收預估較 26H1成長
約 2.5 倍。。需求端能見度已延伸至 2028 年，目前產能只能滿足既有客戶
不到 50%的需求。2027 年產能預計增至目前 3.5～4倍，但依現有訂單需求
仍不足，後續仍有追加設備空間；主要擴產限制來自設備交期。

Ge/Si成長率雖高，但短期對整體EPS貢獻有限。26H1營收占比僅約 1%，
依 26H2 成長幅度及 2027 年 3.5～4 倍成長假設推估，2027 年營收約 3 億

元，占研究部 2027F 營收 68.7 億元約 4%～5%。即使 Ge/Si 毛利率明顯高
於一般 Epi， 基期仍小，2026～27年公司獲利仍主要取決於 8吋 Si Epi營收、
稼動率及毛利率。

擴產效益自 2027年陸續反映，獲利延續成長 
研究部預估 2026 年營收 53.3 億元、年增 37.0%，毛利率 23.1%、營益率
16.7%，稅後淨利 7.60 億元、EPS 2.56 元；2027 年營收 68.7 億元、年增
28.8%，毛利率 25.1%、營益率 19.1%，稅後淨利 10.95 億元、EPS 3.69
元。 
2026 年獲利上修主要反映 26Q2 毛利率優於預期、26H2 8 吋 Si Epi 維持高
稼動率及 26Q3 開始調價；2027 年則受惠 8 吋新增產能陸續開出、GaN 獲
利增加與 SiC 虧損收斂，以及 Ge/Si營收占比提升。 
估值延續採用 PBR評價。嘉晶過去 PBR約落在 2～8x，考量 8吋 Si Epi 供
需改善、Compound Epi 營運轉佳及 Ge/Si中長期成長動能，給予 6.5x 27F 
PBR。6.5x 位於歷史區間偏高位置，已反映部分基本面改善與 2027 年成長
預期，後續評價倍數上修空間相對有限。上調目標價至 152 元(6.5x 27 (F) 
PBR)，潛在上漲空間 14.7%，維持「區間操作」評等。

## Document 113: 4763材料 群益

Source PDF: all report pdf/4763材料 群益.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 10 月 1 日

1 群益投顧 
材料 *-KY (4763 TT)  2Q26 因山東廠停工歲修，毛利率降至  40.9% ， 3Q26 已回升至近  
50%；扣 除 匯 回 美 元 3 億 資 本 之 一 次 性 稅 款 約 新 台 幣 9.6 億元後 ，稅後 EPS 0.72 元 。
2027 年併入  Cerdia 全 球 產 能 後，年 產 能 達 絲 束 16.40 萬噸與醋片  19.50 萬噸 。全
球 絲 束 市 場 由 五 大 業 者 濃 縮 為 四 大 業 者 ， 公 司 採 取 控 制 接 單 策 略 ， 預計  10 ~11 /2026
進行  2027 年 合 約 調 價。

研 究 員 ： 吳 健 鉦   
A51755@cim.capital.com.tw

材料 *-KY 法 說 會 展 望 正 面， 4Q26 將 調 整 2027 年 合 約 價 格：  
材 料 *-KY 於  09/ 21 /2026 宣布以約美元  3.68 億 收 購 全 球 醋 酸 纖 維 大 廠  
Cerdia 100% 股 權 。 交 易 架 構 由 兩 部 分 構 成， 材料 -KY 以 自 有 資 金 支 付 現
金 對 價 約 美 元 2.14 億 ， 佔 比 約  58% ； 其 餘 發 行 新 股 109,295 仟股 ， 佔比
約  42% ， 交 易 完 成 後 賣 方 黑 石 集 團 實 體 將 持 有 材 料 -KY 增 資 後 約  9.95% 
股 權 ， 集 團 股 本 由 9.89 億 股 增 至 10.98 億股。   
被 收 購 方  Cerdia 2025 年 營 收 為 美 元  7.31 億 ， 調 整 後  EBITDA 為 美 元  
2.67 億， EBITDA 利潤率為  36.5% 。 截 至  06/2026 之近  12 個 月 ， 雙 方
合 計 營 收 約 達 10 億 美 元 ， 合 併 EBITDA 達 4 億 美 元 ， 合 併 EBITDA 利
潤率為  39% 。 此 交 易 使 材 料-KY 由 原 中 國 山 東 單 一 生 產 基 地 ， 擴 增 至 德 國
弗萊堡、美國金斯波特、巴西聖安德烈及俄羅斯謝爾普霍夫等全球五大基
地 。 合 併 後 全 球 醋 酸 纖 維 絲 束 設 計 產 能 達 16.40 萬噸 /年 ， 醋 酸 纖 維 素 醋 片
設 計 產 能 達 19.50 萬噸 /年。   
財 務 結 構 改 善 為 本 次 收 購 的 核 心 獲 利 驅 動 力 。Cerdia 目 前 帳 上 存 續 美 元 9 
億 私 債，到 期 日 為 2031 年，票 面 利 率 約 9%，年 利 息 支 出 達 美 元 0.81 億。
管 理 層 規 劃 於  2027 年 交 割 並 表 後 進 行 債 務 再 融 資 ， 透 過 台 系 與 歐 系 銀 行
將 利 率 降 至 4.5% –6% ， 預 估 每 年 節 省 約 美 元 0.41 億 利 息 支 出 ， 扣 稅 後 將
直 接 挹 注 合 併 淨 利 。 
材料 *-KY 2Q26 毛 利 率 降 至 40.9% ，主 因 山 東 各 廠 進 行 3 週至  1 個 月 歲
修 ， 停 工 固 定 費 用 全 數 列 入 當 期 銷 貨 成 本 ，3Q26 毛 利 率 已 回 升 至 近 五 成 ；
3Q26 另 認 列 自 山 東 匯 回 美 元 3 億 資 本 之 10% 一 次 性 所 得 稅， 約 為 0.30 
億美元 (約 新 台 幣 9.6 億元 )， 不 影 響 本 業 長 期 獲 利 。  
產業與產品布局上，全球絲束市場由五大業者濃縮為四大業者。 公司 採取
調控供給策略，將山東部分產能轉向長絲、短絲及不織布，維持絲束市場
供給緊縮以支撐產品價格。收購宣布後引發下游避險訂單，公司採取控制
接單策略，將談判遞延至  10/2026 至  11/2026 進行  2027 年 合 約 調 價 。
產品分工方面，材料 -KY 負 責 長 絲 、 短 絲 及 塑 膠 級 醋 片 ， 切 入 紡 織 、 眼 鏡
框與  AI 穿戴材料； Cerdia 則主打  85 種濾嘴規格及  100% 生物可降解
濾 嘴。材 料 *-KY 具 備 眼 鏡 膠 框 熱 解 回 收 專 利，正 與 東 莞 大 型 眼 鏡 廠 合 作 處
理每年約  1.65 萬 噸 廢 料 ， 預 計 12/2026 正 式 公 告 。

3 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係 「 首 次 」 給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時 ， 其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個
月目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為 四 個等級，定義如
上 。 爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」 或「區間操作」 之投資評
等，就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基
本 面 及 股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ，
研 判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。
我們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來
預測之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投
資 目 的 或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的 ，
既非對投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提
供詢價服務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值
及收益，可能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投
資 來 得 少 的 情 形 。

上品 (4770 TT) 
在手訂單金額創高，營運轉折向上

永豐觀點 
上品接單表現顯著好轉，在手訂單金額回升至 75 億元，公司產能滿載，產品組合
預期持續改善，公司營運轉折向上，研究部轉趨正向看待上品獲利表現。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議上調至買進，主要係因：上品在手訂單金額自年初約 38 億元提升至 75
億元，為歷史新高，反映台灣及中國地區接單表現明顯好轉，考量公司隨著低毛利
訂單出貨完畢，產品組合應逐步好轉，帶動營運轉折向上，基此，研究部預估公司
2026/2027 稅後 EPS 14.3/21.6 元，參考歷史 PER 區間約位於 10-20X，以 2027
年預估 EPS，給予 16X PER 評價，目標價 345 元，投資建議上調至買進。 
上品企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2024 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學)

專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用

### Page 2

2

上品為從事氟素樹脂材料相關製品之生產廠商：公司成立於 1981 年，主要經營氟
素樹脂材料（鐵氟龍）相關製品，包括氟素原料加工之半成品及成品生產及銷售，
下游應用廣泛，是全球氟素樹脂加工應用廠商中少數能同時滿足各階段設備、製
造、研發、創新、設計等完整服務的企業。2Q26 產品營收比重，氟素內襯設備營
收占 68.3%，氟素內襯管件類占 9.6%，氟素應用材料類占 16.7 %，氟素工程收入
占 5.4%；2Q26 公司產品依應用類別區分，半導體占營收 91.3%，化工應用占比
為 6.4%，面板及太陽能應用占 1.2%，其他應用占 1.1%，2Q26 公司營收按地區
別區分，台灣占 49.3%，中國占 44.8%，美國占 3.4%，其他亞洲占 1.0%，歐洲
占 1.5%。 
2Q26 獲利逐步回升：上品 2Q26 營收 13.05 億元（+23.2% QoQ，-0.6% YoY），
毛利率 35.62%（+0.68pts. QoQ，-1.74pts. YoY） ，稅後EPS 3.11 元。上品2Q26
營收回升，主因公司產能利用率提升，且公司因在手訂單金額回升，於 4M26 進
行產能擴充 ， 帶動營收表現增溫 ， 惟公司於2025 因市況不佳 ， 為填補產能利用率 ，
承接較多低毛利訂單 ， 影響公司毛利率表現受到壓抑 ， 但隨著2Q26 產能利用率拉
升，及製成品出貨帶來存貨評價損失回沖，推升毛利率表現已較1Q26 好轉，預期
隨著公司低毛利訂單陸續出貨完畢，及產能利用率拉升，2H26 獲利應持續好轉。

### Page 3

在手訂單金額已顯著回升 ：上品在手訂單金額自年初約 38 億元 ， 至年中提升至60
億元，而至 8 月底已提升至 75 億元，為歷史新高水準，其中，約7 成為台灣地區
訂單，約 3 成為中國地區訂單，美國及其他地區訂單佔比則低於 1 成。2026 接單
槽車數量達 1,000 台以上，優於往年約 600 台水準，反映台灣化學品廠因應晶圓
廠大客戶需求而擴充產能， 對於上品內襯設備及內襯管件需求同步增溫， 而中國記
憶體廠及化學品廠亦持續擴充，下單動能有所增溫。公司於 2025 因市況需求不
佳，為維持稼動率，彰濱廠須承接經統包工程公司發包之低毛利訂單，而嘉興廠也
須面對中國同業削價競爭，影響毛利率表現，隨著市況於2026 大幅改善，除在手
訂單金額顯著回升外，彰濱廠及嘉興廠皆可挑單生產，產品組合預期將逐步好轉，
有利其毛利率逐步回升。 
2H26 產能達滿載，獲利有望持續增溫：展望 2H26，因客戶需求強勁，公司彰濱
廠及嘉興廠於 7M26 已達滿載水準，受客戶拉貨時點影響，目前工廠產值大於公
告營收表現，隨著客戶陸續提貨，應可見上品營收持續增溫。公司先前彰濱廠槽車
約當產能約為月產能 80 台，但隨著 4M26 擴產完畢，及公司持續招工補充人力，
公司產能已提升至月產能 140 台，因預期在手訂單金額將持續增加，公司規劃將
於 2027 年再擴充至月產能 170 台，嘉興廠人力亦提升約 10-12%，以因應客戶強
勁需求。而先前承接的低毛利訂單仍有部分尚未出貨完畢，但因營收比重持續下
降，預期產品組合應持續改善，亦有利毛利率及獲利表現回升。 
營運轉折向上，投資建議上調至買進：展望後市，台灣化學品廠及中國記憶體、化
學品廠擴建，推升對上品氟素樹脂產品需求，上品在手訂單金額顯著回升，公司產
能達滿載，隨著低毛利訂單陸續出貨完畢，產品組合改善，毛利率及獲利表現應可
見回升，觀察上品原料 PTFE、PFA 供需仍緊，報價趨勢於近年逐步走升，然上品
屬原料商 Chemours、Daikin 首要供應廠商，可確保原料供應穩定，又因上品為
氟素樹脂設備龍頭廠商，技術優於日本同業，故可向下游順利轉嫁原料成本漲幅。
研究部先前下調上品之投資評等，主要係因接單表現不佳，在手訂單金額下滑，部
分產品交期延後，公司須承接低毛利訂單填補產能，壓抑獲利表現，然近期已見在
手訂單金額回升，嘉興廠及彰濱廠皆達滿載水準，並可挑單生產改善產品組合，應
有利公司評價回升。研究部預估公司 2026 營收 55.86 億元（+18.2% YoY），毛
利率 36.09%，稅後淨利 11.4 億元 （+40.1% YoY） ，稅後EPS 14.3 元，預估2027
營收 72.71 億元 （+12.3% YoY） ， 毛利率39.29%， 稅後淨利17.28 億元 （+51.1% 
YoY），稅後 EPS 21.6 元，參考歷史 PER 區間約位於 10-20X，以 2027 年預估
EPS，給予 16X PER 評價，目標價 345 元，投資建議上調至買進。

2026/09/30 投資速報

電機機械 
豐祥-KY (5288.TT) 
休閒車輛續強，電子新業務量產，成長動能再升級

營收走勢圖

研究員/張均壕 JUN_HAO1@uni-psg.com

由於北美 Honda 新車型訂單貢獻顯著，帶動 3Q26 營收淡季不淡，加上
電子新業務的訂單開始貢獻，為豐祥下半年營運挹注強勁成長動能， 研
究部據此上修豐祥今年獲利 8%，預估2026 年 EPS 17.17 元(+41%YOY)。
展望 2027 年，預期在休閒車輛、電動機車新訂單以及電子新業務貢獻
下，豐祥明年業績將繼續攀高，預估 2027 年 EPS 21.09 元。研究部給
予 12 倍本益比，將目標價上調至 250 元，維持買進評等。

重點摘要：

1. 電子業務啟動第二成長曲線，2027年進入量產階段： 
豐祥將電子業務視為企業新一輪發展的核心驅動力 ， 當前以AI
伺服器機櫃為主力產品以及其他電子相關品項。 今年下半年已
陸續有新產品少量出貨。在營收比重部分，2026年電子業務約
占合併營收的3~5%。未來隨著客戶需求釋放及新項目量產，搭
配新廠房啟用，創造更多生產空間，公司規畫2H27開始以大規
模自動化模式提升產能， 預期2027年電子業務將有更多的營收
貢獻，更能增添中長期營運動能。。

2. 3Q26淡季不淡，4Q26營收再攀高，年度獲利上修： 
豐祥第三季繼續受惠北美Honda新專案出貨順暢，季度營收 表
現優於原先季減的預期， 呈現淡季不淡 ， 延續前季的強勁表現 ，
年增率保持大幅成長 ； 之後進入越南當地電動機車零件拉貨旺
季，第四季營收有望再度衝高。整體而言，在休閒車輛與電動
機車業務雙引擎帶動下，公司下半年營收動能無虞，本業獲利
維持在高檔區域並保有顯著的年成長動能 ， 加上轉投資事業獲
利成長，研究部上修豐祥今年度獲利9%，預估豐祥2026年合併
營收94.68億元(+39%YOY)，毛利率23.4%(+1.0pct)，費用率
9.4%(+0.8pct)， 預估稅後淨利11.94億元(+41%YOY)， 預估2026
年EPS 17.17元。

3. 三大成長引擎接棒，2027年業績將再創高： 
展望2027年，研究部認為豐祥將繼續保有偏強的營運動能，主
要來自於三大核心領域 ：(1)休閒車輛新訂單持續堆疊與放量 。
特別是受惠於北美供應鏈轉移效益 ， 豐祥對客戶的供應滲透率
提升，北美Honda、Harley-Davidson等客戶陸續有新機種將於
明年量產；亦有日系客戶的新訂單將提前至明年出貨，使休閒
車業務在2027年依然是貢獻整體營收增長的最主要基石；

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

(2)越南當地電動機車與電動汽車市場規模擴大，相關訂單持
續增加，為既有業務提供成長支撐 ；(3)電子新業務的實質貢
獻。豐祥切入伺服器機櫃供應鏈，經過2026年下半年的試產準
備後，2027年將迎來訂單放量，預期電子業務佔營收比重躍升
至5%以上。此外，休閒車輛占比提高，產品組合將繼續優化，
毛利率能再墊高；配合物流逐漸回歸自營管理，費用率亦有望
下降，將進一步提升盈利能力。研究部預估豐祥2027年合併營
收116.46 億元(+23%YOY) ，毛 利 率23.7%(+0.3pct) ，費 用 率
9.1%(-0.3pct)， 預估稅後淨利14.70億元(+23%YOY)， 預估2027
年EPS 21.09元。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

M31(6643 TT)  
台灣股市︱電子上游 
投 資 速 報 
買進-首次 目標價 NT$666 (潛在報酬+36.0%) 2026-09-23

先進製程滲透，推升 IP 與 Royalty    
◼ 結 論 與 建 議 
AI/HPC 與 Chiplet 推升高速 Inter face IP 需 求 ， 公 司 布 局 PCIe Gen 6/7 、
112G SerDes 及 D2D （ 晶粒間高速互連 ），受惠 AI 伺服器高速互連需求成
長。預估 M31(6643 T T)  2026 年 EPS 5.83 元， 2027 年 2/3nm 先進製程
Royalty 放 量 、 占 比 目 標 突 破 20% ， 成 為 營 收 成 長 與 獲 利 率 改 善 主 要 動 能 。
EPS 15.85 元，建 議 給 予 本 益 比 42 倍，設 定 目 標 價 666 元（42X ＊ 2027(F) ），
評 等 「 買 進 」 。

◼ 重點摘 要  
AI/HPC 與 Chiplet 驅 動 高 速 介面  IP 需求  
隨 AI/HPC 、資料中心及 Chiplet 架構發展， AI 系 統 效 能 瓶 頸 逐 步 由 運 算 能
力延伸至 Memor y 與 I/O 頻寬，帶動 PCIe 、 SerDes 、 D2D 及 DDR 等 高 速
Inter face IP 需 求 提 升 ； 大 型 單 晶 SoC 逐 步 朝 多 Die ／ Chiplet 架構發展，
透過不同製程 Die 整 合 提 高 頻 寬 、 良 率 與 設 計 彈 性 ， 並 增 加 Die -to -Die 高
速互連需求。 IPne st 預 估 ， 全 球 高 階 Inter face IP 市場規模將由 2025 年
25.7 億 美 元 增 至 2029 年 49.0 億 美 元 ， CAGR 17.5% ，其中 Ethernet ／ D2D
／ SerDes CAGR 約 21% 成長最快， PCIe 及 DDR 亦約 17% 。

公 司 簡 介 — 全 球 基 礎 元 件 與 高 速 傳 輸 介 面 IP 廠  
M31 主 要 提 供 半 導 體 矽 智 財（ IP ）， 產 品 涵 蓋 基 礎 元 件 、 高 速 傳 輸 介 面 及 類
比 IP ，主要服務全球晶圓代工廠與 IC 設 計 公 司 。 產 品 包 括 標 準 元 件 庫 、記
憶 體 編 譯 器 （ Memor y Compiler ） 、 輸 入 輸 出 介 面 （ I/O ） 、 靜 態 隨 機 存 取
記 憶 體 （SRAM ） ， 以 及 通 用 序 列 匯 流 排 （USB ） 、 高 速 周 邊 元 件 互 連 （PCIe ）、
行 動 產 業 處 理 器 介 面 （ MIPI ） 、 顯 示 介 面 （ Displ ayPor t ） 、 低 功 耗 記 憶 體
介 面 （ LPDDR ） 、 快 閃 記 憶 體 介 面 （ONFI ） 、 通 用 快 閃 儲 存 介 面（ UFS ）、
高 速 串 列 收 發 器（ SerDes ）等 高 速 介 面 IP ， 應 用 涵 蓋 AI/HPC 、資料中心、
網通、儲存、手機、車用及 AIoT 。隨先進製程設計複雜度與成本提升，第
三方 IP 採 用 需 求 增 加 ； 公 司 持 續 由 成 熟 製 程 向 2 ～ 16nm 先 進 製 程 延 伸 ， 並
與 台積電、聯電、 Intel 、 Sa msung 、 GlobalFoundries 等晶圓廠合作。

下半年 成長 加溫  
M31  2Q2026 營收 4.2 億 元 ， QoQ +6.9% ， YoY -6% ，營益率 -4% ， QoQ 
+1 2.8ppts ， YoY -12.7ppts ， EPS -0.0 6 元。 2Q26 營 運 明 顯 改 善 ， 受 惠 營
收回升、雲端運算支出下降及研發資源配置優化，營益率 改善至 -4.0% ，
EPS 收斂至 -0.0 6 元 。 預 估 M31  3Q2026 營收 5.5 億元， QoQ +31.5% ，
YoY +56.4% ， 營 益 率 18.9% ， QoQ +22.9ppts ， YoY +32.5ppts ， EPS 
2.25 元。 1H26 完 成 全 年 原 規 劃 40% 營收 ， 下 半 年 目 標 完 成 60% ， 以 全 年
營收雙位數成長為目標 。展望 2027 年，受 惠 Royalty 持續成長及費用 控 管，
營收與獲利率可望同步改善 。

2nm 完成 Tape -out

16nm 以下 Royalty YoY 近 +400% 、占 Royalty 逾 30% ， 3nm 亦 已 開 始 貢
獻； Fabless 占營收 81% 、 創 近 五 季 高 ， 涵 蓋 智 慧 終 端 、 AIoT 、 記 憶 體 及 車
用晶片，其中 16nm 以 下 授 權 金 占 Fabless 達 68.3% 。 隨 2nm 客戶已完成
Tape -out ，預計 4Q26 ～ 1Q27 進 入 量 產 並 貢 獻 Royalty 。

拓 展 北 美 CS P、 合作 ASI C 業者  
公司由消費性 IP 延伸至 AI/HPC 與 Data Center ，布局 PCIe Gen 6/7 、
112G SerDes 及 高 階 SRAM ，並透過 Design Ser vice 拓展北美 CSP 、與台
灣主要 ASIC 業 者 洽 談 3nm 等 新 案 ； 另 與 信 驊 科 技 深 化 伺 服 器 Inter face 及
Customized  IP 合作，既有合作涵蓋 BMC 及 Cupola 3D 相機，其中
Cupola360 已採用 MIPI D -PHY IP 。

評等說明  
1)  強力買進 / Str on g  Buy ： 我 們 對 該 評 價 個 股 未 來 展 望 正 向 ， 並 預 估 於 未 來  12 個 月 投 資 期 間 ， 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 大 於  5 0 %   
2)  買進 / Bu y： 我 們 對 該 評 價 個 股 未來展 望 正 向 ， 並 預 估 於 未 來  12 個 月 投 資 期 間 ， 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 大 於  2 5 %   
3)  逢低買進 / Tr a d in g  Bu y： 我 們 對 該 評 價 個 股 未 來 展 望 正 向 ， 並 預 估 於 未 來  12 個 月 投 資 期 間 ， 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 小 於 2 5 %   
4)  未評等 / N ot Ra te d ： 至 目 前 我 們 沒 有 足 夠 基 本 資 料 判 斷 該 公 司 評 等 ； 或 此 期 間 康 和 與 該 公 司 有 特 定 交 易 ， 基 於 規 範 或 康  
和 政 策 ， 限 制 予 該 公 司 評 等

免責 宣言 :  
本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 康 和 投 資 顧 問 股 份 有 限 公 司 版 權 所 有 ， 禁 止 任 何 形 式 之 抄 襲 、 引 用 或 轉 載 。 本 研 究 報 告 所 載 內 容 ， 純 屬
研 究 性 質 ， 所 載 述 意 見 可 隨 時 予 以 更 改 或 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 ， 僅 提 供康和 集團內部 同 仁 及 客 戶 參 考 ， 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任何
意 見 ， 不 構 成 ( 且 不 應 解 釋 為 ) 在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 劵 或 其 他 投 資 標 的 之 要 約 、 招 攬 、 邀 請 、 宣 傳 、 誘 使 ，
或 任 何 不 論 種 類 或 形 式 之 表 示 、 建 議 或推 薦 買 賣 本 研 究 報 告 所 述 的 任 何 證 劵 。 報 告 內 容 所 提 及 之各項 業務 、財務 等 相 關 資
訊、 意 見 及 預 估 ， 係取材自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資 料 來 源 ， 既 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 做 出任

Less bull, more bite

◆ Smartphone companies have gone from being demand drivers 
to price takers 
◆ But we think if memory price inflation slows, that could be 
enough for margins to improve before shipments increase 
◆ In a separate report published today, we initiate coverage of 
Xiaomi with a Buy rating and a TP of HKD33.20 
Hope amid hard times. This report looks at what component shortages and higher 
memory prices mean for the economics of the smartphone market. The key message 
is that while average selling prices have jumped as costs surged, handset makers 
don’t need memory prices to fall to recover. Any slowdown in AI-driven memory price 
inflation could be enough for margins to improve before shipments start to increase.  
Stabilisation in 2027, recovery in 2028. IDC forecasts that global smartphone 
shipments will fall 16.7% y-o-y in 2026 – the steepest contraction in industry history – 
decline a further 2.9% in 2027, and rebound 4.5% y-o-y in 2028.  The low base in 2026 
should release some replacement demand but higher handset ASPs and a limited supply 
of low-end memory chips will likely limit the recovery. In China, handset inventories rose 
in 2H25 and 1Q26 before easing in 2Q, suggesting the shock of higher memory costs 
arrived before inventory and demand had normalised. We expect the first green shoots to 
appear in ASPs and gross margin, ahead of shipment numbers. 
Memory inflation hit margins before consumer prices. Spot pricing moves first, 
followed by contract prices, handset makers’ blended procurement costs, component 
inventory consumption, handset bill of materials (BOM) and reported gross margin. 
Lower-cost memory inventory delays the impact on earnings, while finished goods 
and channel inventories delay the ability to reprice. We see 4Q25-1Q26 as the 
margin-absorption phase, with the larger earnings impact likely to run through 2H26.  
Higher costs to be passed on through new products. The main levers for handset 
makers are higher prices for new models, fewer discounts, slower RAM and storage 
upgrades, wider premiums for high-memory models and savings elsewhere in the BOM. 
Times are particularly tough because AI/server demand is tightening memory supply at a 
time when smartphone demand remains weak. In previous cycles, smartphones and PCs 
were major sources of DRAM and NAND demand, so higher memory costs coincided 
with better end demand. This time, handset makers are having to accept supplier-driven 
price increases without the same support from sales volumes.  
MLCC risk. AI servers use more and higher-spec multi-layer ceramic capacitors 
(MLCCs), shifting supply to higher-value products. The effect on handset profits should be 
limited as these key components make up only a small part of the BOM. However, supply 
shortages can still disrupt production – if consumer-grade components are scarce, finding 
replacements takes time. See our Xiaomi initiation report.

Why we think the smartphone market won’t recover in 2027 
The decline in 2026 will not create a strong replacement cycle next year 
According to IDC forecasts made in June 2026, global smartphone shipments will fall 16.7%  
y-o-y in 2026e to just over 1bn units, decline a further 2.9% in 2027e, and then rebound 4.5% in 
2028e when memory supply begins to normalise.  
The low base should support replacement demand, but affordability remains the constraint. Higher 
memory costs are raising the bill of materials (BOM) for handsets and pushing manufacturers to 
either increase prices or reduce entry-level supply. The thinking is that a consumer who defers an 
upgrade in 2026 does not necessarily return in 2027 if the replacement device is materially more 
expensive, or offers less memory at the same price. We think premium markets should be more 
resilient, while entry-level Android demand is more exposed.

Exhibit 2. Global smartphone shipments: 
recovery pushed out to 2028e 
 Exhibit 3. China smartphone inventories* 
were still elevated when memory costs 
began to rise

Source: IDC estimates, HSBC  *Inventory proxy: China’s smartphone production + imports – China’s smartphone 
shipment – exports. 
Source: NBS, GAC, CAICT, HSBC

The first-half vendor data already show this split. Global shipments declined 5.5% y-o-y in 1H26. 
Apple and Samsung shipments rose 5.8% and 8.7%, respectively, while those for Xiaomi, 
OPPO and Vivo fell 22.8%, 13.8% and 13.5%, respectively (source: IDC). Memory is not the 
only explanation: product timing, geography, channel execution and inventory also matter. 
However, the divergence is consistent with stronger resilience at the premium end and greater 
volume pressure in price-sensitive Android segments.  
China’s implied handset-inventory series point in the same direction. Inventory rose through 
2H25 and reached another high in 1Q26 before easing in 2Q26. The series is calculated as 
production plus imports less domestic shipments and exports. It should be treated as a 
directional supply-chain proxy rather than actual channel inventory because the underlying 
datasets use different definitions and timing. The turning point is nevertheless useful: 
manufacturers entered the memory upcycle before the finished-goods cycle had fully normalised. 
Memory pressure extends beyond smartphones, but the earnings sensitivity varies 
materially by product 
TVs have seen a meaningful increase in memory’s share of BOM as prices rose, while 
wearables, audio devices and other IoT products generally carry lower absolute memory 
content and therefore less direct P&L sensitivity. In these categories, selected niche-memory 
availability and channel inventory & pricing can matter more than headline DRAM or NAND 
moves. We are beginning to see signs that pricing momentum is moderating in some consumer 
and niche categories, although conditions remain tight in higher-capacity products. Our sector

call is therefore about the second derivative: slower memory inflation is likely to moderate

Higher memory prices do not directly determine smartphone 
shipments 
Why memory inflation is not the sole cause of the fall in smartphone demand 
Xiaomi’s historical shipment data suggests that the relationship is weak. Mobile DRAM prices 
have moved through several cycles since 2018, while Xiaomi’s quarterly shipments have been 
driven more visibly by product launches and seasonality. Falling memory prices have not 
consistently triggered stronger shipments, while rising prices have not resulted in an immediate 
or proportionate decline in units.

This does not mean memory prices are irrelevant with regards to demand. Consumers do not
replace a phone because DRAM or NAND becomes cheaper, and they do not automatically
cancel a replacement because memory becomes more expensive. Replacement age, macro
conditions, product innovation, subsidies, retail financing and channel inventory remain the main
drivers of unit sales. Memory prices affect demand indirectly once higher component costs

change retail prices, available specifications, or the supply of low-end models.
What is different this time is the source of the price increase. In earlier cycles, smartphones and
PCs were large contributors to incremental DRAM and NAND demand. The same healthy end
demand that tightened memory supply also helped handset makers raise ASP and improve mix.
In 2026, AI servers and high bandwidth memory (HBM) are pulling capacity and capital towards
higher-return applications, while consumer electronics demand remains weak.

TrendForce, an industry research company based in Taiwan, expects overall mobile DRAM price
hikes of around 8-13% q-o-q in 3Q26, given Samsung’s already elevated base in 2Q26. The supply
of low-power double data rate (LPDDR) RAM, a specialized type of computer memory designed to
use less power and take up less physical space than standard RAM, is still tight.
In terms of NAND, TrendForce expects price increases of 10-15% in 3Q26, with contract prices

already at record highs. As a result, we believe that weaker smartphone production may not be
enough to bring memory prices down quickly. AI/server demand can continue to absorb
available supply, leaving handset makers with less bargaining power than in a consumer
electronics-led cycle.
When are higher memory prices reflected in reported earnings?
The simple answer is later than suggested by the spot price chart below (Exhibit 8), and with no
fixed lag. Large smartphone OEMs procure most memory under contract rather than buying

directly in the spot market. Spot prices affect negotiations and indicate direction, but the
relevant cost is the OEM's blended contract price across suppliers, memory generations and
purchase periods.
The transmission path: Spot price → contract price → OEM procurement cost → component
inventory consumption → handset BOM → reported gross margin.
Rolling inventory delays the transmission in both directions. When contract prices rise, lower-
cost DRAM and NAND inventory can temporarily protect reported margin. When memory prices

stabilise or fall, more expensive inventory may still flow through the cost of goods sold (COGS)
before the savings become visible. Existing finished goods and channel inventory can delay the
impact further, as OEMs may need to sell older models before new launches can reset prices
and specifications.
Exhibit 8 is consistent with a lagged but not fixed transmission of memory costs. Mobile DRAM
prices began declining in 3Q22 and Xiaomi’s smartphone margin subsequently improved, while

the increase in mobile DRAM prices from 3Q24 was followed by weaker margins in 2025. This
sequence is consistent with inventory-led cost transmission, but it does not establish a structural
asymmetry or a fixed 2-3 quarters lag. Product mix, promotion, channel inventory and other
component costs also affect Xiaomi’s reported gross margin.
We believe 4Q25-1Q26 likely marked the initial absorption period, when contract price began
resetting but lower-cost inventory still provided some protection. The impact of higher

procurement costs should become more visible in 2H26 as inventories turn, although the timing
and magnitude would depend on Xiaomi’s procurement contracts, inventory position, shipment
mix and product launch schedule. Margins could improve in 2027e if memory prices stabilise
and new products provide greater scope for pricing, specs and mix adjustments.

MLCCs: smaller cost, but a potentially larger operational risk 
Multi-layer ceramic capacitors (MLCCs), the components that store and release electrical 
energy to stabilize voltage, are a key part of the story. AI demand is creating a more selective 
MLCC squeeze than the broad-based memory inflation discussed above. AI servers require 
substantially more MLCC content and a greater mix of high-capacitance and higher-
specification parts than conventional servers. This does not mean smartphones directly 
compete for every component: the AI-grade components where supply is tightest and 
mainstream handset MLCCs often have different specifications. 
Instead, the transmission mechanism is through capacity allocation and supply-chain behaviour. 
Japanese and Korean suppliers are increasingly prioritising higher-value AI and infrastructure 
products, while longer lead times, distributor restocking and precautionary ordering are 
beginning to spill over into some consumer-grade categories. Recent industry data point to a 
bifurcated market – tight high-end supply alongside a broader, but still uneven, tightening in 
mainstream products.  
For smartphone handset makers, the direct earnings exposure is much smaller than for memory 
because MLCC accounts for only a small share of BOM. The operational risk, however, can be 
disproportionate. MLCCs are low-cost but specification- and qualification-sensitive components; 
replacing an unavailable part may require supplier qualification or board-level validation. A 
shortage in a relatively inexpensive component can delay production even when its impact on 
BOM cost is immaterial. 
We view memory primarily as an earnings and affordability bottleneck, while MLCC represents a 
second-order production risk. The key factor to watch is whether today’s tight supply of high-end 
components broadens into sustained shortages of qualified consumer-grade parts. 
Premium brands have more room to pass through costs; mass-
market brands have more margin exposure 
Can Xiaomi pass through higher memory costs? 
We believe the company is unlikely to pass through the full increase without affecting demand. 
Xiaomi’s lower handset ASP leaves a smaller gross profit pool per device, while competition in 
mass-market price bands limits the scope for headline price increases. Xiaomi can partly offset 
the pressure through premiumisation, specification adjustments and lower promotional intensity, 
but it will likely absorb part of the higher cost through smartphone gross margin.
